- [FRONTEND-NUMERIC-MIGRATION-3] Where the bare parse_int or parse_float name is claimed in an ordinary function body, the E_NUMERIC_PARSE repair replaces the whole call with the str method form, `operand.parse_int()` or `operand.parse_float()`, and parenthesizes an operand that is not a name, field, call, method call, index, slice or string. No declaration can shadow that method, so a wrapper named for the builtin, such as the EDA port's `parse_int(s: str)`, no longer becomes a call of itself; the method builds the same Core IR as the bare call.
- [FRONTEND-DEVELOPER-PROJECT-1] Developer typed/IR dumps, coverage and Core checking resolve the same configured module roots, source budgets, embedded-file boundaries and import policies as normal builds; rejected inputs do not emit misleading successful dumps.

- [FRONTEND-CORE-NOALLOC-1] Explicitly written noalloc declarations are warning contracts, not frontend effect ceilings. Check and check-project do not compute the Core allocation contract or reject its violations. Build, run, build --run and test inspect ownership-expanded Core before optimization, including unused concrete bounded bodies, cold blocks and transitive callees; each violated declaration reports W_NOALLOC_ALLOCATES at its allocation witness with the bounded function and allocating callee. Unsupported concrete bodies warn with their reason; unresolved foreign/callback sites are conservative. These warnings do not fail execution. Lint maps them to L_NOALLOC_ALLOCATES in the noalloc-allocates rule, block mode in every profile, so lint --gate exits 3 unless waived with a reason. Cost keeps static may-allocate sites. Stack storage is permitted; compiler allocation and process startup are outside the contract. Explicit effects(...) lists retain guest-level allocation ceilings.
- [FRONTEND-ALLOCATION-SUMMARY-1] A summary request that falls back to concrete bodies after a new or changed written noalloc declaration still publishes fresh summaries on success. A subsequent unrelated edit can stand in unchanged bodies again, with effects, mutation facts and diagnostics identical to a cold check. Validation-only check requests likewise keep publishing after bounded-body edits.

- [NEW-CORE-U2-BORROW-1] A nested record-field borrow may be read from a consuming update’s result when every field in its path is unchanged and the result dominates that use. Direct record updates and internal mut calls use complete field-write summaries; overlapping writes, whole-value replacement, unknown calls, and phi edges retain their original snapshot ownership. No trapping operation moves.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-RELEASE-1] Generated managed-element layouts carry specialized child-release callbacks with constant offsets and outer-to-inner union guards; dictionary callbacks distinguish whole entries, keys, and values. Every live reference occurrence uses the existing runtime count-release primitive and iterative pending chain. Generated, guardless lists with exactly one pointer per element release that child directly through the same primitive, preserving element order without an indirect callback per element. Other visitors and layouts retain their existing walks. Legacy C layouts retain interpreted destruction; publication, shared and saturated counts keep their existing behavior.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-LOX-1] A signed constant addition or subtraction using a phi value or record field is proven safe only when the identical operation on the corresponding value was checked on every incoming edge, or that edge supplies a safe constant. A check from a loop iteration may cover its unchanged backedge by induction, with a separately covered entry; no positive interval is assumed for a mutable frame count. Verify-facts restores the arithmetic check.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-LOX-2] A bounded read-only list projection with one return and no loop may inline its complete control flow, preserving checked arithmetic, negative-index normalization, list bounds checks, and inline trap ancestry.
- [NEW-CORE-U2-FRAME-1] A frame copied from a list remains an independent value across disjoint runtime updates, later writes to that list, and writeback of the frame. A live alias preserves the original fields; a borrowed list-element address may replace the copy only when its entire use interval excludes writes, moves, and releases of its storage.

- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-1] A private iteration over an explicit-separator text split may visit borrowed pieces lazily without an intermediate list, including leading, adjacent and trailing empty pieces. Unicode lengths and separator validation keep their original semantics. A piece that escapes is materialized, and ownership keeps every borrowed source alive and unchanged.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-2] A nonescaping split list may store offset/length pairs and derive borrowed pieces for read-only uses, preserving indexed bounds checks, Unicode scalar lengths and integer parsing grammar. A piece passed to storage or an unsupported consumer materializes before that use.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-3] A whitespace split loop inserting a materialized borrowed key reuses its existing lookup probe on the same dictionary; copy-on-write retains the existing owned-key re-probe rule.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-VIEW-1] Read-only byte-string slices may borrow octet spans with the same negative and clamped bounds as copied byte slices, without Unicode interpretation. Source-update guards and ownership preserve value semantics and lifetime; plain debug retains copies.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SEARCH-1] Forward search and nonoverlapping count share the bounded span search: single octets use memchr and repeated multi-octet candidates fall back to the bounded two-way search. Scalar search windows, empty needles, prefixes, suffixes and replace sizing preserve their original semantics. Replace already sizes one output buffer before copying.
- [NEW-CORE-S2-SPLIT-4] A private split with only fixed nonnegative indexed reads may keep a bounded offset prefix in its function frame. The source-update guard applies, every short-input access retains its original bounds check, and a list length observable outside unreachable negative-index adjustment blocks disallows this representation.

- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-1] Intermediate stage artifacts bind length-delimited inputs to the complete compiler identity and a stage schema. Atomic records include their key and payload checksum; missing, damaged, malformed, oversized, and unavailable entries lose reuse and compute fresh. Verification builds bypass intermediate caches.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-PACK-1] Intermediate stage records are published in complete checksummed journal frames, then sealed into immutable packs with verified indexes and independently bounded compressed blocks. Publication preserves read-your-writes and independent-store visibility; interrupted tails are misses, and only a complete pack replaces its journal. Concurrent writers serialize publication and newer records supersede earlier values for the same key. A successful save attempts sealing after its journal reaches 4 MiB, after releasing publication locks; failed cache I/O leaves the published journal available, while allocation failures remain explicit. Corruption, file loss after admission, invalid lengths, and unavailable storage retain conservative cache recovery. `MYLANG_STAGE_CACHE_COMPRESSION=none` writes raw blocks for diagnostic comparisons; the default `zlib` policy, grouping, stage identities, and reads of either encoding are unchanged, and invalid values fail with a configuration diagnostic. Successful builds flush pending packs before publishing their build-replay receipt, and JSON cache reports expose this final maintenance time separately from stage times.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-2] Function-stage artifacts guard numeric function/type catalogs, normalize literal references and mapped source line/column positions, and restore lists with exact owned capacities against the current source. Earlier same-line length edits preserve following functions' debugger and error-trace locations. Changed file lengths and literal-pool numbering do not invalidate unrelated functions; invalid locations and operand indexes miss safely.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-PACK-2] Packed-stage filename lookup uses a retained index with stable file IDs. Journal payload checksums and pack preparation, compression, and temporary-file writes occur outside the interprocess publication lock. Each bounded pack commit revalidates both its open journal and current path against the captured inode, length, modification/status times and compiler-bound header before publishing. The journal remains available until all chunks commit; damage or replacement during preparation rejects publication, and independent newer records retain sequence precedence. Allocation failures remain explicit and partial files are removed. Deterministic publication counters expose filename lookups, refreshes, unlocked preparation bytes and sequence-lock acquisitions without changing stage keys or block encodings.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-3] Lowering artifacts key a canonical dereferenced checked function tree, the ordered body-independent checked declaration/type catalog including generic instances and inferred summaries, resolved embedded contents, debug dropping and compiler identity. A rolling physical discovery guard preserves function/type IDs without hashing the growing module per body. Catalog and discovery guards normalize numeric lambda source offsets while retaining ordered IDs and signatures; restored bound declarations take fresh checked names and spans, while actual lambda body keys retain their complete names. Replay restores only each function's declaration/type/string discovery delta in order, including unused strings and compiler rendering authority names, relocates local spans and mapped external spans, and runs the existing module transforms afterwards. Damaged or invalid artifacts fall back to fresh lowering with the same refusals; the uncached builder remains an oracle for full IR and LLVM text.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-4] Persistent optimizer function stages execute in the original mutation order and bind bodies, source ancestry, consulted callee bodies, available callee return intervals, caller-derived parameter hulls, effects, mode and pass events. Unchanged stages restore portable IR and retained final facts, ownership, vector plans and exact statistics; ordinary body edits reuse independent functions while direct, recursive and caller-parameter dependencies invalidate their consumers. Exploratory field analysis retains two rounds of sparse per-function reachable writes and type-opening/function-reference evidence. Reuse requires exact input field-table and consulted return identities. Final-stage keys bind the canonical whole-program field table; changed field dependencies restore validated pre-fact checkpoints before recomputation. Uncached passes remain an exact output oracle; malformed artifacts miss and decoded intermediate storage is reclaimed per function. Ordinary optimized development builds without whole-LLVM or statistics output retain validated durable fact handles separately from final IR metadata and decode dense facts only on LLVM unit misses; freshly computed facts remain available in memory even if publication fails. Eager and deferred analysis identities match; cached facts are verified before applying cached IR, and artifacts lost after admission trigger a conservative retry.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-6] Native compilation respects both the requested worker count and a byte admission budget estimated from input size, with one oversized unit permitted alone. Workers use private reclaiming scratch and return diagnostics to the coordinator in input order; they never allocate on its arena concurrently. JSON reports expose stage reuse and inclusive stage latency plus actual linker-process time separately.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-7] Successful ordinary builds release frontend syntax, interfaces and checked trees after lowering and before backend analysis. Physical IR tables remain on the result arena; borrowed function names, literal strings, source paths, line indexes, mapped origins, embedded contents and entry labels are copied there first. Cached and fresh LLVM, source positions and inline ancestry remain unchanged, and allocation failure releases the frontend exactly once.
- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-8] A compact optimizer index preserves exploratory call/return summaries, incoming parameter hulls and final artifact keys for unchanged functions whose post-fusion input and dependency closure match. Ordered leaf-inline, CFG-inline, unswitch and parameter-inline checkpoints retain exact output identities, portable inline ancestry, pass counts and stage-specific call/reference graphs, including nonidentity transformations. A dirty caller observes the callee checkpoint that physical pass order would have produced; identity transformations alias their preceding checkpoint. These functions bypass earlier stage lookups and reconstruction; compact lowering discovery headers admit unchanged raw bodies without decoding instructions, and dirty callers request checkpoint bodies only when inspected. Final IR and analysis remain artifact handles until emission needs them. Demand keys guard declaration layout, semantic lowering identity, and source locations. Bodies with local replay positions bind the current original path, declaration line/column, and exact source prefix through the maximum replayed local offset; that maximum participates in the key. Unsupported nonlocal positions retain conservative whole-file guards, and invalid source bounds lose demand reuse. A missing deferred artifact retries once with eager frontend checking and artifact restoration, repairing the index; repeated failure is an invalid IR error. A reverse raw direct-call/function-reference graph invalidates callers of changed inputs. Changed incoming parameter hulls demote candidates and restore their pre-parameter checkpoint and accumulated pass counts before parameter-dependent optimization. Changed fusions retain portable post-fusion base checkpoints; unchanged fusions alias raw lowering. Compact ordered runtime declaration deltas are replayed only when dirty functions retain their exact ordered helper-creation requests, including requests for existing declarations, and the complete declaration schema and layout. Changed request sequences trigger eager recovery. Unchanged recursive functions may remain deferred when their exploratory summary validates the exact current ordered callee-return inputs. Record-bearing builds validate both field-summary rounds against those inputs and the field-table identity, then check the final field hulls. Changed exploratory inputs resume exact pre-fact checkpoints before recomputation; incoming parameter hulls must also match. Missing summary receipts, changed declaration layouts and missing or malformed artifacts retain the ordered stage-cache path; final IR, facts, ownership, pass statistics and relocated debugger ancestry match uncached optimization.
- [NEW-CORE-DEMAND-1] A development build may omit an unchanged ordinary checked body only when a verified lowering receipt admits its stable declaration, dependency context and source region. The lowering replay still validates its ordered catalogs and discoveries; a reached omitted body whose artifact is absent, damaged or invalid restarts complete frontend checking before fresh lowering. Receipts for nonlocal debug locations additionally prove the touched original-file identities and exact bytes, including locations synthesized during lowering. Bounded allocation-contract bodies and their transitive callees remain concrete for diagnostic checks. Cached and fresh IR, LLVM source positions and warnings agree across body edits, moved sources and effect changes.

- [NEW-CORE-STAGE-CACHE-9] Ordinary optimized-debug builds may pass unchanged LLVM units to the native driver as artifact handles. Compact receipts preserve exact names and runtime dependency flags. A settings-bound receipt locates the original text-derived native object key, including mode, CPU, tool and runtime ABI; the native object checksum is still verified. Only a missing object or explicit cache verification requires reloading LLVM and checking its exact key. Missing or damaged LLVM then triggers conservative recovery; a verified native object remains usable without LLVM. Freshly emitted LLVM remains usable when artifact publication fails.

- [NEW-CORE-R-ARRAY-1] Fixed arrays over four words are indexed in their existing storage. An element write reuses the old array home only when the memory-home planner proves that no live snapshot or operand aliases bytes it overwrites; otherwise the new snapshot is copied once. Index bounds, negative-index normalization, and managed-element ownership remain unchanged.
- [NEW-CORE-R-SORT-1] The new-core native 64-bit integer engine uses insertion for at most 32 values, the adaptive stable merge engine for 33 through 256 values, and stable byte-radix partitions above 256, skipping constant bytes and already ordered inputs. Equal integer words retain their order; signed keys flip their sign bit for ordering. Packed keyed-sort words use the same engine and retain their original-position tie-breaker. Other types and reverse sorting keep the stable merge engine. Scratch allocation precedes every radix write and never allocates managed memory.
- [NEW-CORE-R-SRET-1] Sret and readonly memory arguments retain their separate noalias parameters, including functions passed as values. Destination/input sharing stays disabled after the sole-pointer prototype removed copies but increased record_pass instructions; its measurements and LLVM are retained as experiment evidence.
- [NEW-CORE-R-PUSH-1] Fallible record producers keep ordinary contiguous noalias sret storage. Whole-result reads, differently sized forwarded union variants, wider managed errors, shared or growing lists, and error cleanup after destination-list destruction preserve values and ownership. The payload-destination prototype is retained in experiment history: conservative lifetime checks left parse_list P6 unchanged at 444,833,472 bytes and added 30.70% total EDA record-copy bytes, so the ABI is not enabled.
- [NEW-CORE-R-ARRAY-2] Fixed-array literal elements seed an integer hull, element replacement widens that hull, and SSA joins use the existing convergent aggregate analysis. A read inherits only the proven hull. A normalized index whose interval lies within the fixed type's length needs no bounds trap; verify builds retain the trap and assert inherited scalar intervals.

- [NEW-CORE-JUICE-PLAN-1] Native Juice planning exports normalized selected workload contracts, exact expanded sources and inputs, compiler executable contents, build tools and declared dependencies, measurement configuration, CPU/OS context and expected workspace paths. It creates no workspace, takes no cache lock, builds nothing and executes no workload. Tool identity probes remain permitted. Input scopes include native glob bases and explicit dependency directories; prebuild library identity and undeclared provider closure remain explicitly incomplete.
- [NEW-CORE-COST-QUERY-1] Native static-cost queries reuse final Core function counts only under the current structural catalog, allocation provenance, pass events, reachability and verification mode. Existing compiler-stage queries and successful exact-unit LLVM remarks may be reused under their complete tool and emission identities. Supplied counters and remarks remain live inputs; missing or damaged query records compute fresh, failed remarks remain unknown, and cache verification uses the independent fresh oracle. Current-work reports distinguish computed, reused and verified queries without inferring machine cost or elapsed time.

- [NATIVE-WORK-CLAIM-1] Native compilation and linking acquire exact content-key work claims, recheck completed cache entries after waiting, and release claims on failure or process exit; waiters hold retention leases and independent keys retain parallel work.

- [NATIVE-TEST-COALESCE-1] Concurrent portable native test executions acquire unique exact identity claims in sorted order, recheck receipts and current sources after waiting, and retain claims through publication or failure. Claims retain cache storage without holding publication locks during execution; forced reruns invalidate receipts under the claim before executing so a failed rerun cannot supply an earlier success to followers.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-1] The opt-in `--experimental-zero-rc` prototype moves owning lists, strings, bytes, buffers, and their aggregate fields. Calls borrow; `mut` is exclusive; independent copies duplicate dynamic children. Snapshots, escaping views, resources and foreign calls are rejected in stage 1. Default and earlier single-owner modes retain their semantics.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-2] Admitted zero-RC values are destroyed directly, including runtime arguments and nested children. Static text uses a storage-kind tag rather than a count. Text growth, owned slicing and explicit copies preserve values without any reference-count reads, writes or uniqueness tests, in debug and optimized builds.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-3] Zero-RC compilation admits only audited runtime helper contracts. Surviving IR retains/uniqueness tests and emitted count operations reject compilation. Transitional runtime count primitives trap if reached; whole-execution counters and leak checks include startup and teardown.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-VIEWS-1] Under `--experimental-zero-rc`, text and octet owners produce nonowning `TextView` or `ByteView` ranges with `.borrow(start, end)`. View bindings are immutable; reads and copies cannot follow owner mutation or movement. A function returning a view has exactly one immutable view parameter and returns a range derived from it. Views cannot be stored inside aggregates. Creation, further borrowing, length and scalar-byte reads allocate nothing and perform no RC; `.copy()` materializes independent owned storage. Unoptimized and optimized builds enforce the same source contract.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-CONTAINERS-1] Zero-RC dictionaries and sets own their keys and values. Insertion moves inputs, reads borrow, replacement drops the old value, and pop transfers the removed entry. Explicit copies recursively duplicate owned children, including active union/option payloads and recursive boxes. Pattern bindings borrow their matched owner and cannot escape it. Recursive copies and destruction use finite type descriptors and iterative runtime worklists, without reference counting.

- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RESOURCES-1] Zero-RC scoped files borrow their backing provider through normal and early-return cleanup. Scope cleanup completes and destroys the handle while the provider remains live, and result adapters explicitly transfer owned payloads out of their temporary boxes. Root providers own their state without reference counting.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-PAYLOAD-TRANSFER-1] In the zero-RC prototype `try` consumes its Result/Option and transfers the active payload without an implicit deep copy or retain. Recursive payloads are loaded into an independent value home before freeing only their box shell; their owned descendants move with the value. The rule covers success, propagated errors, options, loop-carried values and mutable-call writebacks. Borrowed pattern bindings remain borrowed and explicit `.copy()` still creates independent owned storage. The internal consuming extraction is rejected under the ordinary RC ABI.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-MATCH-TRANSFER-1] In the zero-RC prototype a `match` directly on a call, callback invocation, variant construction, collection `.copy()`/`.pop()`, or a built-in text/byte decoder, scalar counter or numeric parser consumes that fresh union and transfers its active payload binding. Compiler-generated expression blocks preserve this classification. Bindings can move into returns, locals and containers exactly once; ignored payloads are destroyed. Matching a named value or a field/index projection continues to borrow, even at its last use. Destructured record fields remain projections and cannot move independently. Checking and lowering share this syntactic rule; no last-use inference or ownership annotation is required. Ordinary RC builds retain their existing match semantics.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-RESOURCE-SCOPES-1] In the zero-RC prototype a dependent Filesystem, Network or Environment capability is bound by `with parent.dir(...)`, `with parent.host(...)`, `with parent.read_only()` or `with parent.only(...)`; the parent must be a named local. The scope borrows the parent and prevents moves/replacement through every exit. Scoped capabilities and files cannot be moved or returned. Cleanup consumes the child while borrowing its parent, innermost first, including return, `try`, break and continue. Root capabilities own their provider; dependent capabilities own only their copied scope bytes. Scoped read-only files release their handle without requesting completion rights. Direct C extern calls remain scalar-only; native provider callbacks borrow call buffers, successful acquisition transfers one handle, successful completion consumes it, and release consumes an uncompleted handle. Zero-RC propagation transfers an owned active payload from its Result/Option wrapper (NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-PAYLOAD-TRANSFER-1).
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-TASKS-1] At the experimental Core task boundary, a scoped group receives each capture box by move. A task borrows its capture during execution and publishes an independent owned result; completion destroys the capture. Taking a result transfers it out of the group. Ending or dropping a group joins all pending tasks before destroying untaken results and handles. The group cannot escape into a capture. The legacy borrowed-capture and retained-result helpers are not admitted in zero-RC mode. Scheduler synchronization atomics remain; memory ownership uses no reference counts. Source task syntax and borrowed task captures are outside this initial backend prototype.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-TASKS-2] The reserved `parallel.map(values, function)` standard function borrows distinct input elements for scoped tasks in zero-RC mode. Callbacks accept a read-only input and return an independent storable value. Map preserves input order and joins every task before returning the owned output list; no handles or borrows escape. Ordinary builds use a sequential implementation. Unit elements and general closure captures are outside this prototype.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-ABI-1] The zero-RC runtime ABI has no reference-count field: its kind occupies byte offset zero, and alignment keeps the complete header at 32 bytes. Its runtime excludes legacy retain, release, publication and count-dependent COW implementations at compile time, and poisons their identifiers to reject accidental references. Emitted LLVM contains neither RC declarations nor operations. Static storage uses a kind bit. Unique owners are destroyed directly; allocator and scheduler synchronization remains permitted.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-CACHE-1] Zero-RC lowering, optimizer and emission caches bind the ownership mode and applicable owned/shared type semantics. Generated deep-copy identities bind type structure. A cached native object carries the zero-RC runtime selection even when its LLVM text is not loaded. Warm builds must preserve uncached output and RC-free behavior. Source ownership checks remain eager; earlier single-owner/snapshot experiments continue to bypass Core caches.
- [NEW-CORE-ZERO-RC-LIBRARY-1] Admitted text/byte helper calls borrow inputs and return independent owned results or immutable static characters. Slicing, trimming, case conversion, splitting, joining, replacement, formatting, hexadecimal conversion and UTF-8 decoding use no retained aliases. Environment stand-ins copy their backing name/value bytes. Unadmitted standard-library operations are compile-time refusals, never RC fallbacks.
